# BACKLOG — GeotechStaffEngineer, planlens, geotech-references

THE one list of open work across the three repos: the app, `planlens` and
`geotech-references`. Owner's rule, 2026-10-12: earlier sessions kept starting
narrower to-do lists, and whole plans dropped off. There is no other to-do
list:
- HANDOFF §0a-current names only this week's slice, by ID.
- Plans, design notes, TRACE_REVIEWs, smoke REVIEWs, field-feedback FINDINGS and `LIVE_TEST_QUEUE.md` keep the detail and the evidence.
- Every open item in those documents has a line here pointing back to it.

## How every session uses this file

1. **Starting work.**
   - Read the owner's answers on the **Backlog Desk** (https://claude.ai/artifact/9M7saxwayRQ6c7v1TeDjyQ: `ArtifactData` list of collection `responses`) and fold any new ones in here (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/README.md`).
   - Read the Monday drift canary's latest report (`RemoteTrigger` `list_runs` on the routine `geotech-drift-canary`, then `get_run_log` of the newest run) and fold any breakage or version past `webapp/version_guard.py`'s ceilings in here (INF-12, INF-03).
   - Then read §0 and the tier you are working in, and say which IDs you are taking.
2. **While working.** Anything new goes HERE the moment it is found, under its area with the next free ID: a bug, a gap, a follow-up, an unverified claim, an owner question, a fix list from a review or a field session. The next free ID is the highest number in use for that prefix in this file or in `BACKLOG_DONE.md`, plus one (`.venv/Scripts/python module_work/backlog_dashboard/sweep_done.py --next GM` prints it); never reuse one.
   - One line per item: what it is and why it matters, then `*Src:*` the document holding the detail.
   - A detailed write-up elsewhere is fine. A to-do list elsewhere is not.
   - **No edge-case chasing (owner, 2026-10-27).** A follow-up that a fix's builder, reviewer or check turns up becomes its own item only if it changes a value in the saved reports' outputs (or what a live run returns). Otherwise it is one "noted, not fixed" line in the parent item's Done entry: keep note of the issue; the fix is not critical. The owner: "We need to stop chasing edge cases... In 6 months, we'll probably have a whole new set of core LLMs, and those edge cases might be caput anyway." Report ingest starts no new floor follow-ups (the rule-based readers) until the model-first measurement is in (RI-87, RI-168). Later is a pool of ideas, not debt. Owner questions on a technical point (like the specific-gravity hydrometer question) are still welcome; record the answer, and code it only if it changes a result.
3. **Finishing an item.** Delete its line and add it to "Done since the last sweep" with the commit. If a §0 item changed, update HANDOFF §0a-current's slice too.
4. **Releasing.** Every item the release closed moves to Done. Raise or lower tiers for the next train. Then sweep: `.venv/Scripts/python module_work/backlog_dashboard/sweep_done.py --note "app master <sha>"` moves the whole Done list, text unchanged, to the top of `BACKLOG_DONE.md` under a dated heading. Sweep on demand too, whenever the Done list grows long.
5. **A full sweep** (on the owner's ask, or every few weeks): re-read the plan documents against this file, as on 2026-10-12. Triage the Later and Parked items into a file (keep, merge, close as covered with the commit, or ask the owner about a drop) and apply it with `module_work/backlog_dashboard/apply_triage.py`, as on 2026-10-20. Then run `sweep_done.py` and record the date below.

After any change to this file, rebuild and republish the Backlog Desk (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/build.py`, same URL). Every `[owner]` item needs a plain-English card in `module_work/backlog_dashboard/context/`; the guard test checks this.

`webapp/tests/test_backlog.py` (part of the gate) checks:
- the IDs are unique, and each has a tier;
- every ID cited here exists, open or closed (in Done or in `BACKLOG_DONE.md`); every ID cited in HANDOFF §0a-current is open;
- no closed ID is reused, and `BACKLOG_DONE.md` holds only swept entries;
- CLAUDE.md and HANDOFF still point here, and every `[owner]` item has a desk card.

**Key:**
- **Tiers:** **Now** = the current focus, named in §0. **Next** = the next train. **Later**. **Parked** = the owner decided to wait, or (2026-10-27, the owner's rule against chasing edge cases) likely too narrow to implement: kept as a note, not planned; each says why. RI-168 re-reads report ingest's Parked and Later items once the model-first numbers are in.
- **[owner]** = needs the owner: a decision, an action, data, or CfA.
- *was:* = the item's id in an older list: HANDOFF `A1`–`E6`, or "Where things stand" `0`–`3e`. That old text is in git history at 414525a.
- **Short names:**
  - `H` = `HANDOFF.md` (line numbers as of e329b22)
  - `FI` = `module_work/FUTURE_IDEAS.md`
  - `B5` = `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-10-08_foundry_5.33.0rc1/` (Foundry brief 5 TRACE_REVIEW and slices)
  - `LTQ` = `module_work/LIVE_TEST_QUEUE.md`
  - `RA` = `module_work/REVIEW_ARCHITECTURE.md`
  - `RH` = `module_work/REVIEW_HARNESS.md`
  - `HT` = `module_work/harness_theory/`
  - smoke reviews = `module_work/live_smoke/runs/<wave>/REVIEW.md` (gitignored)
- **Last full sweep:** 2026-11-04: all 380 open items triaged by four read-only helpers against the owner's order of work (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/triage_2026-11-04.json`): 33 closed as covered and 26 merged (each with its evidence in Done), 32 re-tiered with a reason, 39 DROP? and 31 ASK put to the owner, the rest kept; the eight CfA asks merged into OWN-44 as one message. Before that, 2026-10-12 (app master 414525a, planlens f92541d = v0.13.0, refs c53e587 = v1.4.1).
- **Last Done sweep:** 2026-11-10, for release 5.35.0 (136 entries to `BACKLOG_DONE.md`, app master 972ffad). Before that, 2026-11-04 (339 entries, before the full sweep of the same week).
- **Last tidy:** 2026-10-27: the 296 Later items triaged against the owner's rule against chasing edge cases (three read-only helpers, one yardstick): 125 to Parked with a reason each, 170 kept in Later, INF-30 closed as already complete. Before that, 2026-10-20 (app master 116ddb1): Later and Parked triaged (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/triage_2026-10-20.json`: 15 merged, 6 closed as covered, 9 to the owner as OWN-69), then the Done list swept to `BACKLOG_DONE.md`.

- **OWN-143** [Later] [owner] **Geotechnical judgement calls made by the lead on 2026-11-12: revisit**. **Owner, 2026-11-12:** "Go through backlog: if anything is waiting on a geotechnical-engineering-judgement decision from me and is holding up coding work downstream, go with your recommendation but make a 'Later' backlog item with the decision for me to revisit in future rounds." 31 calls, each with its own answer on the card (27 of 2026-11-12, the rest added from the builds of 2026-11-13). **6 unblock work:** the slope plan's ten steps, those that change a factor of safety first (OWN-101, GM-140); slope surfaces with m_alpha below 0.2 kept, flagged and located, with the Bishop-OMS check (OWN-102); reverse-curvature circles closed by the crack EM 1110-2-1902 C-10c prints, a deeper overhang discarded (OWN-103); the cos-squared correction for a sloping water table built, off by default (OWN-104); downdrag's headline neutral plane kept at the intersection, the settlement-compatible plane beside it in the summary (OWN-105); a recompute printout that gives only gamma' run as the submerged case (OWN-130, DR-11). **10 defaults change** (GM-310, GM-311): a drilled shaft's rock base with no RQD refused, naming the inputs that settle it (OWN-84); MSE backfill with Cu below 4 refused without a tested F* (OWN-85); passive wall friction under log-spiral only for a layer marked clean sand or gravel (OWN-82); the stiff-clay p-y curve from 48 kPa (OWN-68); the aggregate-pier stress ratio by column type, 2.5 or 5 (OWN-108); Liao and Whitman's SPT overburden correction capped at 2.0, Peck's offered (OWN-112); seismic passive wall friction phi/3 (OWN-114); the small-group uplift cut below 5 driven piles (OWN-118); Meyerhof's depth factors from his own table at every depth (OWN-131); moment-curvature EI for lateral piles to be built (OWN-19). **11 confirmed as built:** cantilever wall sliding by GEC-6 (OWN-73); the step between UFC's Eq. 4-12 and its charts (OWN-79); a clay's alpha in downdrag from UFC's chart (OWN-83); Jeanjean (2009) soft clay offered and marked unchecked (OWN-106); an underconsolidated layer run as given with its warning (OWN-107); braced excavation support loads by the hinge method (OWN-109); allowable bending stress 0.60 Fy (OWN-110); each wall tool's own moment basis (OWN-111); Idriss and Boulanger's lower residual-strength curve (OWN-113); a rigid slab rounded up to the next 1/2 inch (OWN-115); the base uplift's moment counted as overturning (OWN-117). Each module states the call in its result and offers the other reading (the changed defaults once GM-310 lands). Two depart from the lead's rule and are the first to look at: OWN-104 (the safe side kept as the default although two manuals print the correction) and OWN-131 (the print followed although it is the less conservative reading). **Supplied 2026-11-13:** the owner's pages of Duncan, Wright and Brandon (2014), section 6.5.3 (pp. 91 to 93) and all of chapter 8 (pp. 159 to 168), kept local only, so slope step 8 no longer waits on the owner. **Still the owner's:** getting Jeanjean (2009, OTC 20158) so that p-y criterion can be checked. **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-13 (GM-140 step 1, 6cb96ec):** where two layers are given covering the same ground, the first one listed holds it and the result lists the overlap; ground that no layer covers is refused. No page held prints a rule for either (desk card part 28). **Added from the pile group fix, 2026-11-13 (GM-319):** `pile_group_simple` adds the product term to GEC-12 Eq. 8-79 where the piles are not symmetric about their centroidal axes (no reference held prints the general form; basis statics), and `pile_group_6dof` turns a battered pile's stiffness to the global axes in full without the EM page in hand (desk card parts 29 and 30). **Added from the slope build, 2026-11-13 (GM-140 step 2, 3ed1e62):** slope runs where a method has no solution: Monte Carlo counts such realisations, leaves them out and gives the probability of failure bounded both ways; FOSM gives no result and names the variable; the yield acceleration halves its largest trial kh until the method solves. No printed rule was found for any of the three; each is stated in the result as the module's own (desk card part 31). **GM-322 (2026-11-13), the lead's call:** FOSM and PEM refuse a bounded normal or lognormal variable only when its bounds cut off more than 1e-6 of its probability (both tails together, per variable); at or below that they run on the untruncated variable and say how much was cut. Revisit if a different tolerance is wanted. *Src:* `module_work/gm322/CHECK.md`. *Src:* `module_work/owner_calls_2026-11-12/CALLS.md`

- **RI-103** [Next] **Strata as the narrative defines them, not the log's lines** (OWN-95, owner 2026-10-26; some firms say 'layer' for the same unit). **Background done 2026-10-26** (11 public reports from several kinds of preparer in four countries, 26 borings; private notes `raw/strata_conventions_2026-10-26.md`): every narrative unit boundary fell on a log line (0 of 26 borings without one), but 18 of 26 borings had lines inside a unit; 9 of 11 firms draw a line at every change of description (even within one USCS group), 2 draw lines only at their strata and write smaller changes as notes ('gray below 3.5 ft') with no line; none draws at USCS changes as a rule; 3 of 11 logs label every interval's unit (a stratum column, a geologic symbol, a model-layer number), 3 tag some intervals (FILL, TILL), 5 none; 5 legends say the lines are approximate and transitions may be gradual; line styles are rarely defined; units can repeat down a hole. Evidence thin outside the US. The rule: (1) the log reader records every drawn line as an interval boundary (depth, elevation, the description as printed, classification), and a change written inside an interval with no line ('wet below 7 ft', 'as above, but brown') as a depth-tagged note, not a new interval; never merges intervals while reading; line style and extent kept where printed; the answer sheets follow the same rule (open sheets R04, R21 first; a blind sheet, R34, corrected by a helper that reports only how many rows changed); (2) a stratum label goes on the record only where the log prints one, kept as printed with its source, and a label carried down from one printed above is marked as carried; (3) mapping each interval to the narrative's units is a separate model step, one boring at a time, each interval to one unit or none; unit boundaries snap to the log's lines, and a narrative boundary with no line is flagged; evidence in this order: the log's own labels, a per-boring unit-depth table or a profile figure, per-boring depths in the narrative's text, then site-wide depth ranges with a description match (marked as the model's judgement); model-facing, under model-first, measured before use, after RI-101's first live sheets. **Parts (1)(2) built 2026-11-13 behind `GEOTECH_INGEST_LOG_INTERVALS` (off; merge d80890c, after build j; cloud session, reviewed, review fixes 14ba4f0):** with the switch on, the model-first log reader gives one layer for every interval between two drawn lines and never joins two; a change written without a line is a note on its interval, with its depth where one is written; the line's kind only where the legend or the line's own label says; a stratum label only where printed, with where it is printed and a carried mark; new optional `Layer` fields, left out of a record when empty and out of the DIGGS file; the logs' prompt version gains `+li`; the scorer reports the new groups apart from today's totals and scores a note by its words; the answer-sheet keys are in `module_work/ri103/CHECK.md`. Off, prompts and records are unchanged (digests and snapshot untouched). **Left:** measure it live with the switch on (next Foundry brief; the private answer sheets of R04, R21 and one held-out report need the new keys first: not yet written, they are the owner's files); then the narrative's units over these intervals (the rest of this item).

Checked against foundry-platform-sdk 1.111.0's source and a local fake of the datasets API (`module_work/inf38/CHECK.md`). With no viewer identity every viewer shares one folder. **Left:** the FDE confirms on Foundry the host, `File.list(path_prefix)`, `Transaction.create("UPDATE")`, `File.upload(transaction_rid)`, commit, abort, `File.content`, `Branch.get`, `Transaction.get`, the token's dataset scopes and whose token it is; then the new app wheel, measured on a published app. Noted, not fixed: report ingest's mirror sends one transaction per file (same result, slower); a long history slows the list; no per-folder link. **2026-11-03 afternoon, the AI FDE's test from a published app (kept locally in `../foundry_handoff/gse_app_2026-11-03/FDE_STORAGE_TEST_2026-11-03.md`, never committed):** the platform SDK route merged in eeff9dd cannot work in an app container: the public datasets API through the local data connection answers 403 `EndpointDisabledForContainers` and the public host does not resolve, so scopes are not the issue. What works is the workspace dataset library `foundry.transforms.Dataset`, by the dataset's workspace alias (import the dataset in the Data panel, read and write, and push): `upload_directory` from `/foundry/outputs` saves a batch in one transaction (about 5-7 s for 3-20 files), `files()` lists the whole dataset (3-4 s, no prefix filter), each `File.download()` about 3-4 s; one open transaction per branch (`SimultaneousOpenTransactionsNotAllowed`); overwrite works and earlier files are kept. The app cannot ask who it runs as (401), the token is opaque, and writes show as the publisher, who was also the only viewer. **Rebuilding the backend on that library** (branch `wip/inf38-transforms`), then build h's storage part is replaced by a new delivery. **Owner, 2026-11-03:** GeotechStaffEngineer on Foundry is for the owner alone for about the next year, so the store is built for one user (the shared folder); per-viewer folders and whether writes run as the publisher or the viewer wait until someone else uses it. **2026-11-03 evening, rebuilt and merged (4779f31; commits 0387586, b28fef5, 3b0826a; reviewed, no fixes):** on Foundry the store uses `foundry.transforms.Dataset` by the workspace alias in `GEOTECH_FOUNDRY_CONVERSATION_DATASET` (a resource ID, an undefined alias or a missing library is named with its fix); the platform SDK path and its transaction abort are gone. A turn's changed files go in one `upload_directory` from the outputs folder, in the background and in order (the turn no longer waits 5-7 s), under a lock, a busy dataset retried for about 30 s; files the app did not stage there are never uploaded or deleted (then file by file); restore downloads six at a time through the path guard. On Foundry a sidebar block says "Conversations are NOT kept across restarts" with the reason and fix when no dataset keeps them; the past-conversations list loads when asked for, on every host. Single user (owner, 2026-11-03). Check: `module_work/inf38/CHECK.md`. **Left:** the FDE's round trip in the published GSE app (build i). Noted, not fixed: parallel downloads and whether `upload_directory` skips names starting with `_` or `.` are untested on Foundry (in build i's round trip); a file torn by the next turn writing during a save is replaced by the next save; the "saving" caption stays until the next rerun. Also noted by the review: the background save writes the folder name into `meta.json` while the next turn may write it too, so a title or turn-count change can be briefly lost; two processes sharing the outputs folder would not share the lock. **2026-11-03 night, build i on Foundry (hand-back in `module_work/field_feedback/2026-11-03_foundry_build_i/`, raw gitignored):** the published app saved its first conversation to the dedicated dataset through the alias (five files in one write about 7 s after the turn, in the background; Sync now "0 uploaded, 5 up-to-date"; a feedback entry saved in about 6 s without a turn). Also: the new check's finer-slicing half (`_SLICE_AGREE = 0.5`, `search.py:~266`) does almost nothing; accepted best surfaces still move a lot between 30 and 40 slices (V-043 DE 1.092 → 1.286, V-028 DE 0.733 → 0.817, a weak seam 1.362 → 1.629), so a test comparing a random search against DE compares against a slicing artefact; the 2 % axis tolerance is a heuristic, not from a reference. Measure on the published noncircular problems and the F61 geometry. *Src:* GM-57 report and re-review, 2026-10-16 Also (GM-122 build, 2026-10-17): the noncircular generators waste trials on surfaces that pass into the air and back (the weak-layer search refused 30 of 150 once GM-122 refuses them); generate only surfaces that stay below the ground. w11 (2026-10-17, `module_work/live_smoke/runs/w11-judgment/TRACE_REVIEW.md`, local): the edge warning says "on the edge" for an entry point that lies outside its window; say the surface starts in the air outside the window. First do GM-131 (slices straddling a polyline's corners), which may explain the results that move between 30 and 40 slices. Also (GM-131 review): V-043's differential-evolution best (1.234) sits above the module's own converged critical circle (1.213) and a 41-point polyline inscribed in it (1.2145), so the search falls short; its median comparison is a strict xfail naming this item. Also (GM-138): on V-043 the circular search now finds 1.186 (1.190 on 480 slices), tangent to the soft clay's base, and Slide2's own noncircular search 1.188 with 56 kN/m matches it, while our noncircular search's 1.234 sits 4 % above the circular result; a noncircular search should never end above the best circle it could start from. **Deferred to GM-140** (owner 2026-10-18). **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Noncircular slope search leftovers deferred to GM-140 by the owner.

- **GM-308** [Later] **fem2d footing capacity on frictional soils is limited by the iteration count** (GM-306 review and fixes, 2026-11-05): on the default 40x20 mesh the c-phi footing reads 455.6, 477.3 and 499.0 kPa at max_iter 1000, 4000 and 16000, still rising, so today's frictional answers are lower bounds (the warning says so). (a) A faster nonlinear iteration than constant stiffness (GM-305 found a tangent iteration too slow for the rigid footing) would raise saved frictional answers towards the converged value; (b) check first whether the default domain (half-width 5B) truncates the mechanism for phi of about 31 degrees or more (the Prandtl-Reissner passive zone reaches 4.79B at phi 30, 5.04B at phi 31; not verified by runs). *Src:* `module_work/gm306/CHECK.md` §8. **(b) done 2026-11-06 (merge; cloud session, notes only, read by the lead):** no truncation: at a fixed large max_iter (8000, 16000) and a fixed element size, 5 B and 7.5-15 B agree within one load step at phi 30 and 35 and at phi 40 without a surcharge; at phi 40 with q = 18 kPa 5 B reads 3.5-5 % *below* the wider domains (the safe side); the psi = 0 mechanism's heave lies within 1 B of the centre. No held manual prints the mechanism's extent or an FE domain size (GEC-6 Fig. 5-3, NHI-06-089 Fig. 8-13 draw the zones only). Code unchanged (`module_work/gm308/CHECK.md`). **(a) done 2026-11-06 as a negative result (cloud session, reviewed, review fixes; notes only on master, `module_work/gm308/CHECK_A.md`):** Anderson acceleration of the constant-stiffness iteration and the other schemes tried met the goal on no machine for every case; with the option on, Linux read far below the default on four of eleven cases (B = 1: 196.7 against 321.9 kPa) and answers near collapse varied by machine, so the code is not merged (kept on branch `claude/gm-308-footing-iteration`). The obstacle is the plateau steps: below collapse they stall at a residual near 1e-3 against tol 1e-4 (Mohr-Coulomb face/edge/apex switching), and the settled test turns that into a max_iter-dependent answer. **(c) left:** a footing convergence test that does not depend on max_iter (e.g. a fixed iteration window for the displacement test) or a residual tolerance matched to that noise; it would move saved frictional answers, so measure it on GM-306's 15 cases on two machines first. With (a): frictional answers still rise 3-7 % per doubling of max_iter at 16000 and depend on the load-step size; re-run `module_work/gm308/run_width.py` at phi 40, q = 18 once (a) converges, since a surviving gap would raise saved high-phi answers by about 4 %.

### Module audit, 2026-11-12: what the agent is told against what the code does
Stage 1 (ten read-only scouts, every severity-A finding re-opened by a coordinator): 32 A, 152 B, 186 C; the write-up, the finding tables by module and the plan for stage 2 are in `module_work/module_audit_2026-11-12/AUDIT.md`. slope_stability was read for the record only (hold GM-140).
- **GM-326** [Next] **subsurface: trend fits and missing CPT values said** (module audit, 2026-11-12): `compute_trend` fits any `trend_type` but "log_linear" as a straight line and labels it as asked. With fewer than 2 points it returns slope 0 and R-squared 0. The log fit drops non-positive points but reports the full `n_points` (subsurface_characterization/statistics.py:150-195). Refuse the unknown type (or GM-317), refuse fewer than 2 points, and count the dropped points. The GEF reader turns every missing or non-finite qc, fs, u2 or Rf into 0.0 with no count (subsurface_characterization/formats/gef.py:160-164); keep them missing and report how many. *Src:* module_work/module_audit_2026-11-12/AUDIT.md §4 subsurface_characterization
- **GM-327** [Next] **dxf_import: drawing units and silent defaults** (module audit, 2026-11-12): text annotations are not converted from the drawing units (dxf_import/parser.py:200-223). `units` defaults to metres and is never compared with the drawing's own $INSUNITS, which `discover_layers` reads (parser.py:63, 102; discovery.py:96), so a drawing in mm gives a section 1000 times too large. Missing soil properties become gamma 18, phi 0, c 0 and E 30000 with nothing said (funhouse_agent/adapters/dxf_import_adapter.py:172-182, 236-247; contrary to converter.py:9-11). Nails get 25 mm bars, 150 mm holes, fy 420, bond 100 and 1.5 m spacing the same way (converter.py:194-220). List every default in `defaults_used`, refuse missing strengths, and warn when the header units differ from `units`. *Src:* module_work/module_audit_2026-11-12/AUDIT.md §4 dxf_import
- **GM-328** [Next] **Calc packages: sample packages rebuilt by script, the calc helper told of the worked examples, fem2d's package** (module audit, 2026-11-12; the owner's question the same day: "I see the github folder of them basically hasn't been updated. ## 6. Document Review harness and eval suite

### Default page fixes

Testers get the page's default build (`GEOTECH_REVIEW_AGENT` unset); it took the plan's Phase-1 fixes from the lean build (DR-01 to DR-03), and the lean build itself went with DR-39.

- **DR-43** [Next] **Document Review page: dead `sandbox:` links, and steps that only update the to-do list** (EV-42; `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-11-12_foundry_brief7/TRACE_REVIEW.md` section 10, F-11, F-17): (1) an answer links a file it wrote as `sandbox:/mnt/data/...`, which goes nowhere for the user (15 Sol runs of brief 7 by a text search, one reader's count); the note after `write_docx` and the other saves lacks the line `method_call`'s note has (`core.ATTACHMENT_NOTE`): add it, or point such a link at the attached file of the same name; (2) steps that only update the to-do list took 25 to 42 % of a short task's input tokens on both models, because each step sends the whole conversation again: measure the share over all 84 saved runs first; whether to change the page's `write_todos` rule is then the owner's call, and not before the next model's run (the owner, 2026-11-12: nothing fitted to this model). **2026-11-13 (part 2 measured, no model call; `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-11-12_foundry_brief7/TODO_STEPS.md`):** over the 84 saved runs, steps whose only tool call was `write_todos` took 13 % of all input tokens on GPT-5.6 Sol (16 of 123 M) and 25 % on GPT-5.4: a fixed four to five such steps a run whatever the task, so 20 to 30 % on the 15-sheet single-question tasks and 3 to 5 % on the 40-sheet ones; about four minutes of a 100-minute set-up. It holds on any model, so the orchestrating session added it to the cloud round as task H (`module_work/cloud_briefs/DOCREVIEW_2026-11-13.md`): a setting, off by default, under which the agent updates its list with its next tool call, with a suite arm so the next standing run measures it; no default changes. Part 1 is that round's task C. **2026-11-13 (part 1 merged with TA-37, master 3f51191; cloud task C):** the note after a saved file tells the agent to give the file name, not a `/tmp` path or a `sandbox:` link, on Document Review and the geotech page. ### Review architecture (M2–M4 and the owner's questions)

- **DR-44** [Later] **The drawing-geometry layer as a fallback the agent can reach for** (owner, 2026-11-12: "I can really see it being useful on certain kinds of old as-builts and complex drawings where a conversation with a reviewer is showing that the initial path isn't working"): planlens' geometry layer (`planlens.ir`: leaders, dimensions, callouts, title blocks and revision clouds read from a sheet's vector lines) has not been on the page since DR-39 removed its switch (no suite run showed a gain, on a suite then at its ceiling). The idea: offer it as optional tools known only through their own descriptions, as `find_like` is, so the agent can turn to it when reading text and looking have not answered and the user says so; never a step of a workflow. It reads vector drawings only: a sheet plotted from CAD whose lettering is drawn as lines is its case; a scanned paper as-built has no lines to read and stays with looking and `find_like`, and each result should say which of the two a sheet is. Not before there is a test that could show a gain: a set with stroke-lettered sheets, and a task in which the user refuses the first answer (the coordination tasks are one turn each). *Src:* owner 2026-11-12

- **DR-38** [Later] **Let the page's main model see an uploaded image itself, behind a new switch** (the old `GEOTECH_VISION_INLINE`, lean build only, went with DR-39; the inline machinery stays in the core's `aec_agent_core/deep/inline_images.py`, `aec_agent_core/inline_store.py` and the `minimal` build) (chart suite, EV-35, 2026-11-08): on Sol, Document Review as shipped scores 84 of 99 charts against 95 for the chart with a crop tool and Python, and every page loss traces to the vision side call's text (copied, re-asked, or answering a reworded question); the main model never sees the chart. Today `analyze_image` shows an image inline only for a working-folder file opened by path (`aec_agent_core/vision_tools.py`), so an attachment's first look is always a side call, switch or not. Fix: show an uploaded image to the main model inline when that switch is on. **Owner 2026-11-09: no rerun; not built until a measurement is wanted.** *Src:* `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-11-08_chart_suite_ev35/TRACE_REVIEW.md` §§2, 6 **2026-11-10 (deployment review):** the first design change to try for a stronger vision model: coordination checks (does this callout match that detail) cannot be judged from two separate descriptions written by a side call; keep side calls for bulk transcription. *Src:* `module_work/docreview_review_2026-11-10/REVIEW.md` §3.2 **2026-11-10 (built, off by default; not measured):** behind `GEOTECH_VISION_SHOW_ME=1` the page's `analyze_pdf_page`, `render_region` and `analyze_image` take `show_me`. A look with `show_me=true` runs no vision call: the image is shown to the agent's own model at its next step (the core's `deep/inline_images.py`, in the request only, the newest two views), an uploaded picture too; without it a look is today's vision call, tiles and all. The model chooses per call, and the tool descriptions say when (to judge with its own eyes; not to have a page read out). The reading helper has it as well. Off, nothing changes: the argument does not exist and the page fingerprints are untouched. Suite arm `show_me`. Left of this item: the measurement (EV-40), then the default. Each call carries the newest two images until the turn ends, so the arm's token cost is part of the result. *Src:* `packages/aec-document-review/tests/test_review_show_me_offline.py` **2026-11-13 (brief 7, EV-40):** offered in the `show_me` and all-three runs, used in none of the 70 Sol runs (no call passed `show_me=true`), so the arm measured nothing; not run again as an arm until the record counts `show_me` looks (INF-75). *Src:* `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-11-12_foundry_brief7/TRACE_REVIEW.md`, the arms

### Markups and checks

- **DR-30** [Parked] **The markup check returns a corrected box for rings and rectangles only**; callout, note and highlight looks are not asked for one (asking would change how their area check is measured). Also: the correction count runs per marked copy, not per turn, so a later "try again" meets the stop note until a clean check resets it. Live (F03): two boxes cut the note they mark but passed, because the check tests the note's centre; when a box is confirmed but cuts the thing, return the box the look found. **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Hardens the markup check for more look types and a case seen once (F03); better models likely make it moot.
- **DR-31** [Parked] **`annotate_document` and `open_document` count a copy's markups differently**: the tool said the copy holds 6 markups where `open_document` counted 12, because each label is its own annotation. By arithmetic; the misread rate is not measured. **2026-11-13 (the note is kept; master 6b92058, with VIS-31 part 1):** a tiled page read keeps the `legibility` note while any tile's lettering is under the line, names those tiles with whole-number pixel heights, and says so where the height is the assumed lettering size because the page has no text to measure (`vision_tools._tile_legibility`); the note goes only when every tile reads. The base for DR-07. *Src:* `module_work/docreview_review_2026-11-10/REVIEW.md` §2.5, §3.2 **2026-11-10 (the index and the callout check are built, opt-in; not measured):** planlens main d7f43d8 (after 0.15.1, unreleased): `Document.sheet_index()` reads each sheet's number, title, revision and view marks from its title block, by the place the numbers share across sheets (`planlens/document/sheets.py`), and `Document.cross_references()` pairs each callout with the sheet it names (`crossrefs.py`). Two tools, `sheet_index` and `check_cross_references`, for a host that asks (`ReviewHost(toolkit_options={"sheet_tools": True})`); every existing tool list and result is unchanged. In the app they are behind `GEOTECH_REVIEW_SHEET_TOOLS=1`, off by default (suite arm `sheet_tools`; `packages/aec-document-review/tests/test_review_sheet_tools_offline.py`), and do nothing on the pinned planlens 0.15.1. An independent review ran in three passes before the merge; its rule is in the code: a callout is `unresolved` only where the sheet is in the file and no mark for that view was recognised on it, `not_in_document` where no sheet of that number was read (a finding only if the file is the whole set, which the agent judges from the cover's index), and `cannot_tell` wherever the text does not settle it. On the coordination sets it flags exactly the planted callouts. **Still open here:** a look for sheets with no text layer (scans get no index), the index returned at open, a sheet number accepted wherever a page is, scale and stable markup ids in the rows (PL-27), and `printed_numbers`, which still reads only the top and bottom of a page. Limits as measured on made-up sets only: planlens `planlens/document/DESIGN.md`, "Sheet index and cross-references". First model run: EV-40.

## 10. Tiny Apps, Funhouse and the app split

- **Rollout model (owner, 2026-10-12):** testers get ONE deployment, GPT-5.6 Sol or higher, for every call; not the pilot's `tinyapp-gpt-medium` (GPT-5.1, 768 px). The app measures whatever answers on first use (`funhouse_agent/vision_probe.py`), and planlens maps `gpt-5.6` to `openai-high` / `openai-original`. Sol has run the suite at full resolution on Foundry (5.32.0rc3; B5 39/42).

Also (was TA-04, merged 2026-11-04): Also (was TA-04): the D2 budget message, busy retries, restore after restart, the Excel writer, two testers saving at once, annotate_dxf in real CAD, and D3. **2026-11-10 (deployment review):** also whether a reasoning-class deployment needs `reasoning_effort` (none is sent; the per-answer caps of 4,000 to 6,000 tokens were sized on Sonnet, and a tile read that spends its cap on reasoning returns empty or cut off, never cached, so every repeat pays again).

- **TA-24** [Later] **Busy ladder, the leftovers**: deepagents' summarization makes its own model call inside a before-model hook, outside the ladder; the vision side-call ladder is still 3 tries at 2 s and 4 s, which may be too short if a tokens-per-minute limit is the cause. Set `GEOTECH_TOKENS_PER_MINUTE` once the rollout deployment's limit is known (live checks: whether the gateway's 429 carries retry-after / x-ratelimit headers, and whether 15/30/60/120 s clears its window). **2026-11-10 (deployment review):** the model client's own retries (4, at 180 s each) run inside each try of this ladder and inside the vision ladder, so one stuck call can hold a turn 15 to 20 minutes before the tester sees an error, and hold one of the eight vision slots about as long while other testers' looks queue with no message. Keep one ladder: client retries at 0 or 1, at most one retry of a timeout, and a bound on the vision queue wait; decide on the live check above. **2026-11-13 (one ladder, merged with INF-75, master fd61ee2):** the model client asks a request again once, not four times (`tinyapps_engine.DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES`; `GEOTECH_PROMPTER_MAX_RETRIES` still overrides); a timeout is asked again at most once in all, client and ladder together; a 408 or 504 counts as a timeout only after a try of 20 s or more (`vision_engine.SLOW_TRY_S`, a judgment, not a measurement), so a gateway that answers one quickly keeps the whole ladder; the wait for a reading slot is bounded (`GEOTECH_VISION_SLOT_WAIT_S`, 600 s). What is left: the live check of the rollout gateway above (it decides the ladder's waits, the vision ladder's tries and `GEOTECH_TOKENS_PER_MINUTE`, and shows whether rate-limited page readings now come back "not read" more often; `module_work/LIVE_TEST_QUEUE.md` check 15), and deepagents' summarization call, still outside the ladder. *Src:* `module_work/docreview_review_2026-11-10/REVIEW.md` §2.1

### The app split (Document Review leaves this repo)

- **INF-26** [Later] **Split Document Review from GeotechStaffEngineer** (owner 2026-10-20): Document Review becomes its own package (on PyPI as `aec-document-review` since 2026-11-10; the owner chose PyPI on 2026-11-09, INF-33), installed in Tiny Apps; GeotechStaffEngineer runs as its own app in Palantir Foundry. **Plan merged 2026-10-20** (`module_work/APP_SPLIT_PLAN.md`, commit `aaf93ef`): three packages of our own from this repository (working names: `de-docreview`, the Document Review app, marked so PyPI refuses it; `docreview-core`, what both apps run, public on PyPI like planlens; GSE on PyPI as today); 16 steps after a step 0, each merged and tested on its own, guarded by a test that builds the Document Review agent with every geotech package blocked and by fingerprints of both pages' agents. Decided: the geotech page leaves Tiny Apps (owner, desk 2026-10-20 08:33); the testers' agent moves unchanged, `lean` decided later by DR-05 (lead, the owner can overrule). Owner decisions: OWN-72 (names, Tiny Apps route, CfA questions 6a-6d), OWN-74 (shared code), OWN-75 (release first), OWN-76 (public or private), OWN-77 (figure helper and plotting code), OWN-78 (notice and SharePoint folder). Steps: INF-30 to INF-35. *Src:* owner 2026-10-20 **Owner 2026-10-20 (after the first Foundry run):** Document Review is Tiny Apps only; Foundry only if Tiny Apps fails after its six-month pilot, and not planned around. So the split stands: two hosts with different rules (Foundry: Claude, no SharePoint, its own download and storage limits; Tiny Apps: GPT, SharePoint, CfA's pins). Order (lead, same day): the host fixes from the Foundry run (INF-36 to INF-39) go into today's package, into the shell that step 10 moves to the core; steps 3-8 (INF-30, INF-31) go on; steps 9-16 (INF-32 to INF-35) wait until the Tiny Apps tester start is set and the Foundry wheel route is known (OWN-80), as each new package adds a release. **2026-10-21 (INF-48):** plan rewritten for OWN-74 and OWN-76. Document Review becomes private source in the GHE wrapper repo (no package, no wheel; superseded 2026-11-09, a PyPI package, INF-33). A thin public core goes on PyPI (about 37,900 lines; the owner's three named parts about 21,100). The document tools that only wrap planlens move into planlens (PL-29). Each app keeps its own chat page (about 12,900 lines, copied once at step 13). Steps 1-8 stand (step 7 now prepares the copy); steps 9-16 are rewritten: 9 planlens (PL-29), 10-11 the core (INF-32), 12-13 its release and Document Review's private home (INF-33), 14 the wrapper (INF-34), 15-16 GSE lets go and notes (INF-35). Still gated: OWN-72 (the name before step 10; CfA's 6a before step 14), OWN-78 (before step 14), OWN-87 (before step 13).
- **INF-33** [Later] **App split, steps 12-13 (the core's first release; Document Review's package)** (INF-26 §4): 12, release the core, then GSE pinned to it exactly (planlens already released by PL-29); GSE still carries both pages, so testers are unchanged; exit: GSE on Foundry from the new wheels (INF-25's list); 13, the copy: Document Review's files (plan §2.2), its copy of the chat page (§2.9) and its Tiny Apps host files, its tests and 38 suite tasks become the public package `aec-document-review` (import `aec_document_review`) in `packages/aec-document-review/`, published to PyPI by its own tag workflow (`publish-document-review.yml`) as planlens is, so Tiny Apps installs it from Nexus; a wheel installed by path is the backup (owner 2026-11-09: "We can do PyPI. If it doesn't work, wheel is backup."; this replaces the private repository of 2026-10-21); its own CI job (Python 3.11 and 3.12, the fleet pins, nothing geotech installed); the one deliberate fingerprint change (`plot_data` without `reference_chart`, `plot_code` without the chart lines); from that day review-page changes go there only, and this repository's review files are frozen by a test until step 15; exit: the package installs and runs the review page with nothing geotech present. Releases pre-approved (owner 2026-11-09: "I give you pre-permission to release everything associated with this split push"): the core, `aec-document-review`, any planlens release a step needs, and the GSE release that pins the core; the lead releases when a step is merged, reviewed and green, and tells the owner after. **For step 13 (from step 11 (c)'s review, 2026-11-09):** the core still names GSE in three places a Document Review user sees or records: the run record's app-version lookup (`geotech-staff-engineer`), the default Word and markup authors, and some `runtime_check` messages; the page hands in its own name and package (`module_work/inf32/CHECK_STEP11C.md` §12). **Step 13 merged 2026-11-10 (9411d1a, cloud session, reviewed; fixes d2ad659 to 0e3a37b):** `aec-document-review` 0.1.0 in `packages/aec-document-review` (nothing geotech; pins `aec-agent-core==1.0.0`, `planlens==0.15.0`); the core at 1.0.0 names no application (`app.details`), GSE's output unchanged; the deliberate fingerprint change in the package only; GSE's review files frozen by hash (`webapp/tests/test_review_freeze.py`); CI job `document-review`; no tests in either sdist (`webapp/tests/test_sdists.py`); the package picks up REF-64's deployment instructions (its minimal build does not, and is never the Tiny Apps default). `module_work/inf33/CHECK_STEP13.md`. **Released 2026-11-10 (pre-approved):** `aec-agent-core` 1.0.0 (tag `core-v1.0.0`), `aec-document-review` 0.1.0 (tag `document-review-v0.1.0`; PyPI refused the first upload for an unlisted classifier, nothing uploaded, fixed in 2237b31 with `webapp/tests/test_classifiers.py`; a clean venv installs it from PyPI with nothing geotech) and GSE 5.35.0 (tag `v5.35.0`, notes HANDOFF §0a-current item 0). **Open:** step 12's exit, GSE on Foundry from the new wheels (INF-25's list; the FDE and the owner). *Src:* INF-26 plan, rewritten 2026-10-21 (INF-48)

So CfA's answer to 6a-6d is a file drop on dosdev. Noted, not fixed: routes A and B do not read `packages.txt`, which CfA's guide calls the install list (ask with 6a-6d whether their deploy reads it); nothing builds `app_src/` yet (step 13). **Step 14 merged 2026-11-10 (47db5a3; cloud session, reviewed, its diff put onto master as b181798 with fixes 82a9eb9 to 486037b):** the template installs `aec-document-review==0.1.0` with the core and planlens pinned exactly and pandas==3.0.2; `app.py` imports `aec_document_review.tinyapps_entry`; a `.gitignore` keeps `.env` and `.venv` out of the wrapper repo; `tinyapps/TINYAPPS.md` is for Document Review alone, with the owner's numbered first-round checklist and the platform team's asks (ask 4 names the shared and the owner's own model key); the wheels route installs and serves the page end to end (`module_work/inf34/CHECK_STEP14.md`). **Open:** the owner's round on the test machine (OWN-29), once the three packages reach Nexus.
- **INF-76** [Next] **The shared core and Document Review release as one unit** (owner, desk 2026-11-12: "Yes, one unit"; was OWN-141): `aec-agent-core` and `aec-document-review` take one version number and one tag that publishes both wheels (today `core-vX.Y.Z` by `publish-core.yml` and `document-review-vX.Y.Z` by `publish-document-review.yml`, each pinned exactly to the other's release); GSE pins that one version; `test_docs_currency` and the release steps in CLAUDE.md follow. No code moves, and nothing changes for testers. Do it with the next release of either package. After it, in this order: INF-35 (GSE lets go of its copy of the page and the alias modules), then INF-71's single binding of planlens' tools. planlens stays its own library. **2026-11-12 (from INF-74's review):** the next release of GSE or of Document Review must go out with a core release that has `foundry_dataset_store` (INF-74 moved it into the core): both apps import it and both still pin `aec-agent-core==1.0.1`, which lacks it, and `publish.yml`'s guard checks only that the pinned core exists on PyPI. Installed against 1.0.1, each would keep nothing permanently on Foundry, without a word. **2026-11-13 (from the merges of TA-39 and TA-40):** this branch holds the host notes, so with it `tinyapps/TINYAPPS.md` (its key section), `tinyapps/wrapper_repo/README.md` and the package README take TA-39's paragraph on the exact secret name (`module_work/ta39/CHECK.md`, "For the lead" 1; the sentence "A name longer than 127 characters is never looked up" is no longer whole), and `tinyapps/wrapper_repo/.env.example` and `tinyapps/TINYAPPS.md` list `GEOTECH_SHAREPOINT_MAX_UPLOAD_MB` (TA-40). The release note joins the two items' notes, with the SharePoint folder rule as TA-40 states it, and what OWN-147 answers. **2026-11-13 (from the merges of TA-37, TA-05, INF-75 and PL-39):** the host notes on this branch also take the new settings (`GEOTECH_STOP_BUTTON`; `GEOTECH_SPEND_ESTIMATE` with its three price settings; `GEOTECH_VISION_SLOT_WAIT_S`) and the new defaults (a page reading given up at 90 s, the model client asking again once), which are documented beside the code only. The testers' release also needs a planlens release that carries PL-39, with the fingerprint and tool-spec steps in `module_work/pl39/CHECK.md`, "For the lead". The review of this branch found one thing outside it, for the owner: OWN-149. *Src:* OWN-141
- **INF-80** [Next] **Foundry with several people at once: bind each turn's tools to its own conversation there too** (2026-11-13, from OWN-152): the page's protections for a process that serves several people (tools bound per turn, the working folder always the conversation's own, reads kept inside it) switch on only when a sign-in header names the caller (`identity.multi_user`, `core._shared_host`, `_fileio.turn_binding_required`). On Foundry no header arrives, so with two people's turns running at the same moment a file one asks for can be saved into the other's conversation, as `docs/FOUNDRY.md` section 6 says. With about five people that changes what a saved conversation holds. Turn the per-turn binding on for a Foundry deployment, with no names needed and no refusal of unnamed viewers (that stays the App Service's rule); keep the conversation list shared, as the owner chose. *Src:* OWN-152; `docs/FOUNDRY.md` section 6, "Two people at the same time"
- **INF-79** [Later] **Foundry: telling viewers apart** (2026-11-13, OWN-152): no request header reaching a published app names the viewer, the platform's check of the request found no sign-in token on it, and the container's user is the publisher, so every viewer shares one list of conversations and every save is made by the publisher's account. The owner accepts that for a group of about five. Separating viewers needs Foundry to pass the viewer's identity into the app: a question for the FDE and the platform, then the page's existing per-person folders (as on Tiny Apps). *Src:* brief 8's hand-back, 2026-11-13, part A
- **INF-78** [Later] **A Google model (Gemini) on Foundry: a third engine family** (2026-11-13, OWN-151; from brief 8's hand-back, 2026-11-13, part B): the FDE found that a Gemini model is reached from an app container only through the language-model proxy's Google door (`.../proxy/google/v1/models/<model>:generateContent`, the container's token), in Google's own request and response shape: not OpenAI-compatible, and `palantir_models` has no Google class. Images go as inline data; every image costs a fixed 1,089 tokens whatever its size (256 at low resolution), so small print on a whole sheet is lost unless the page is cropped or tiled first. Tools work, and an image returned by a tool must sit inside the function response's own parts; the model's thought signatures must be sent back with its turn; reasoning is set by a thinking level, not a budget; token counts come back, with no rate-limit headers and no token-counting call; a request may be up to 50 MB. What a build needs: a chat model class for that shape in the core (`engine_config.py` knows `anthropic` and `openai` on Foundry today), the vision engine's image budget for a fixed-size model, and a standing-test run before anyone uses it. *Src:* brief 8's hand-back, 2026-11-13, REPORT_B (private)
- **INF-35** [Later] **App split, steps 15-16 (GSE lets go; notes)** (INF-26 §4): 15, after step 14's exit or the owner's word: the frozen review files, the review profile, GSE's Tiny Apps entry and the two-page default leave this repository; the reverse boundary test (GSE builds with no review file present); a GSE release; 16, one release later: the import aliases out; every doc, notebook cell, Harness Map entry and helper prompt swept to the new paths; BACKLOG items tagged with their home (planlens, the core, GSE, Document Review). **For step 16 (from step 11 (a)'s review):** `funhouse_agent/chart_eval/runner.py` finds the engine module by its old name, so it changes with the callers when the aliases go. **Also for step 16 (doc review, 2026-11-10):** planlens' `planlens/review/tests/capture_app_specs.py` imports `funhouse_agent.deep.vision_kit`, an alias; it should import `aec_agent_core.deep.vision_kit` before the aliases go, or a re-capture fails. *Src:* INF-26 plan, rewritten 2026-10-21 (INF-48)
- **INF-71** [Later] **Document Review after the split: code this package never runs** (deployment review 2026-11-10; a pool of ideas to take with INF-35, when the files are being touched anyway): one folder per conversation (no Working folder box, no `meta["working_dir"]`); new files found by the folder's before-and-after, not by searching tool text for `'saved':`; one path-inside-folder function for five copies; the conversation's folder passed to the turn; one retry ladder (TA-24); one pass per turn in place of the pattern-matched auto-continue; SharePoint by the Graph route only (the Funhouse SDK builders, the Foundry dataset branches and the rename machinery out; upload by path); the page shell's two-page machinery, the budget panel and other hosts' diagnostics out; the `minimal` measuring build out of the tester package; feature detection for an exactly pinned planlens, the second copy of the tool notes and the second page argument out. *Src:* `module_work/docreview_review_2026-11-10/REVIEW.md` §3.1 **2026-11-10:** one of the pool is done, the one whose failure was silent: the reading helper is declared whole in the package (`review_build.READER_NAME`), no longer started from a spec imported from a private name of the agent library, which an upgrade could move and leave the page with the stock helper and every tool. The rest change what runs on the testers' host (the folder model, what counts as a new file, the auto-continue, the SharePoint routes, the page shell) and stay with INF-35, when those files are being moved and a tester round can follow.

- **GM-323** 2026-11-13 (merged with GM-322, same branch and checks): `sobol_analyze` and `morris_analyze` read `n_samples` and `n_trajectories` and refuse a count that does not fit the data or data that is not a whole design; a Sobol Y that fits both designs with `n_samples` left out runs and says in `warnings` and `choices` which design was taken. The expression compiler takes the reliability engines' `safe_expr` limits. No index changes for data that fits its design.
- **GM-322** 2026-11-13 (merged from `fix/gm322-review`: the cloud build, an independent review, a round of fixes; the whole suite green on GitHub). reliability: FOSM and PEM refuse a normal or lognormal variable whose `lower`/`upper` cut off more than 1e-6 of its probability (they ran on the untruncated moments without a word); bounds that cut off nothing run as before, and a cut of at most 1e-6 runs untruncated with the mass in `warnings` (the lead's call, in CHECK.md). Monte Carlo counts the realizations whose g is not finite and refuses a probability when more than 5 % are; with no failures (or only failures) the 95 % interval is the Clopper-Pearson bound, no longer 0 to 0. pystra: NUMBERS CHANGE for a Monte Carlo run that used to stop early at a 5 % COV (it now runs every sample asked for; `seed` is local to the call, and a seeded pystra 2.x run saved before cannot be repeated with the same seed); a failed SORM returns null second-order values and `sorm_failed`, where it returned FORM's values under the SORM names; an unconverged FORM runs no SORM; `converged` is pystra's own flag; with no failures `beta` and `cov_pf` are null. No pf, beta or moment changes for a FOSM, PEM or reliability Monte Carlo run without truncation or dropped realizations. Detail: `module_work/gm322/CHECK.md`.

- **TA-38** 2026-11-13 (with TA-37, master 3f51191; always on, a repair): the page's model-call budget covers the whole turn. An automatic "continue" no longer starts a fresh budget, and the calls that finish a cut-off reply count against it: with the default budget of 40, the most the main agent can do in one turn goes from 118 model calls to 40 in all (in the 84 saved runs of brief 7 the most used was 17, the median 13). The lead's decision: it ships as is, with no higher ceiling and no setting, because the budget is the tester's money. A helper's continuations count inside its own 14 calls; the review suite's `minimal` arm keeps its per-pass count, so its saved runs do not change. A failed call while finishing a cut-off reply keeps the text already written (this one reaches the geotech page with the core), and a turn whose budget ran out on an empty reply says so, and what it did, instead of "(no answer text)". Not built: a ceiling on a turn's image calls, TA-43. Noted, not fixed: a failed continuation of a cut-off reply leaves no event on the activity log; with `GEOTECH_CITED_PAGES=retry` (off by default) the page check's note after a budget-ended turn reads as after a retry.
- **TA-05** 2026-11-13 (with TA-37, master 3f51191; setting `GEOTECH_SPEND_ESTIMATE`, off by default): with the setting on and token prices set (`GEOTECH_SPEND_INPUT_USD_PER_MTOK`, `GEOTECH_SPEND_OUTPUT_USD_PER_MTOK`, optionally `GEOTECH_SPEND_MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD`), a host without the Funhouse SDK shows each tester an estimate of their own AI spend this month, from their own run records. - **TA-40** 2026-11-13 (merged from `fix/ta-40-review`, master 7b97279, and its two page edits with `fix/ta-37-review`, master 3f51191; cloud task D with two rounds of review fixes; the whole suite green on GitHub both times; unreleased): on the Graph route the save after a turn runs on one of four background workers, so a turn ends when its answer is written and one person's large first save holds nobody else's turn; the sidebar shows "saving the latest turn…" and then the result. Uploads go by path: a first save of 40 files is about 45 requests where it was about 300. A large file is sent in pieces from disk, and a failed piece is taken up again. A file above 2048 MB (`GEOTECH_SHAREPOINT_MAX_UPLOAD_MB`) is not sent and is named: an error once, then a note at every sync. A file whose name SharePoint refuses is saved under a nearby accepted name, with `sp_names.json` beside `meta.json` so a restore gives the name back. Each person's folder key has a SharePoint owner folder of its own (`sharepoint_store.owner_folder`): a sign-in name with an underscore in its domain, a doubled underscore, a dot at an end or more than 64 characters takes a new folder at its next save, with MOVED.txt left in the old one; every other tester's folder stays where it is. The Funhouse SDK's SharePoint route keeps the save in the turn. Noted, not fixed: what a person who moves left in an old shared folder stays there, listed by the person who keeps that folder (the host's administrator moves it); a save still queued is lost when the process is killed, and goes at that conversation's next turn or "Sync now"; a held-back file's note keeps the limit it was first refused under; a restore or a download reads a whole file into memory; a failed large upload leaves its upload session to expire; a path over 400 characters still fails at every sync; GSE's frozen copies (`webapp/sharepoint_store.py`, `webapp/graph_sharepoint.py`) keep the old behaviour, shared owner folders included, until INF-35. Live checks on a real tenant: TA-42. The page edits: "Sync now" queues the save wherever saves run in the background (on Foundry too: the click no longer waits for the save) and the status lines follow it; a list of past conversations that could not be read says why, with "Refresh to try again.", and an empty one says "Nothing mirrored yet.". Check `module_work/ta40/CHECK.md`.
- **INF-75** 2026-11-13 (merged from `fix/inf-75-review`, master fd61ee2; cloud task B with its review fixes; the whole suite green on GitHub; unreleased; one new setting, `GEOTECH_VISION_SLOT_WAIT_S`, default 600 s, 0 for no bound): the run record (`activity.jsonl`) gains four events: `side_call_timeout` (a page reading given up at its limit, and whether it was asked again), `side_call_late_end` (when the abandoned call ended and how long it held its slot), `engine_retry` (each retry inside the Foundry engine) and `vision_slot_timeout`; and a per-turn fingerprint of the offered tools' wording, not their names only. The gateway check that decides the ladder's waits stays open as TA-24.

- **GM-318** 2026-11-13 (merged from `fix/gm318-review`: the cloud build, an independent review, a round of fixes, a re-check; the whole suite green on GitHub): a driven pile, a drilled shaft or a laterally loaded pile whose toe is below the soil layers given is REFUSED, with the depth the layers do not cover. Before, it ran with only a Python warning the agent never saw (axial and drilled: no side resistance below the profile and the toe in the last layer; lateral: no soil resistance below the layers), so a saved run of such a pile now stops until the layers reach the toe or `extend_last_layer` is set. With `extend_last_layer` the last layer is carried on to the toe, and the result, each row of a capacity-against-length curve that stands on it, the soil tables and the calc package say so (the capacity is then that of a profile given to the toe, higher than the old silent answer by the side resistance below the profile). Lateral p-y layers must start at the ground surface: stickup is the scour model, the soil taken as removed (GEC 9 Sec. 6.7.5, p. 91); soil that stays in place and is to resist nothing is given as a top layer of negligible strength. `capacity_vs_depth` for driven piles lists the trial lengths it could not run, with a short reason; a reinforced-concrete pile's cracked-stiffness iteration reports whether it converged; `max_ei_iterations` below 1 and a `profile_depth_given` deeper than the profile are refused. No value changes for a pile inside its profile. Follow-up: GM-341. Detail: `module_work/gm318/CHECK.md`.
- **GM-319** 2026-11-13 (merged from `fix/gm319-review`: the cloud build, an independent review, three rounds of fixes, a re-check and its confirmation; the whole suite green on GitHub). The three audit points: the elastic settlement of the equivalent footing is a tool, `equivalent_footing_settlement` (UFC 3-220-20 Eq. 6-54; it takes M, or Es with Poisson's ratio, and has no default for either); `pile_group_6dof` turns each battered pile's stiffness to the global axes in full; `pile_group_simple` refuses a pile entry with a batter or a stiffness and returns no cap displacement (null, where it returned zeros). NUMBERS CHANGE in three cases, each stated in the result. (1) A 6-DOF group with battered piles: forces and displacements move (made-up group: 386.4 kN, as statics gives, where the tool gave 715.7); vertical groups are unchanged. (2) `pile_group_simple` with piles not centred on the origin: x and y are measured from the piles' centroid, where the tool takes its loads (x = -1, 1, 3 m, Vz 900 kN, My 200 kN-m: 250 / 300 / 350 kN where it gave 281.8 / 318.2 / 354.5, which did not sum to Vz); the result gives the centroid and the moment a load at the origin needs added. (3) `pile_group_simple` with piles not symmetric about their centroidal axes: the product term is included (3 x 3 less a corner: maximum 400.0 kN where it gave 392.3; 2 x 3 less a corner: 506.7 where it gave 552.4), matching the 6-DOF method at equal stiffness. A centred, symmetric layout keeps its numbers. Refused now: a moment about the one line every pile is on (the simple tool returned equal shares as if the moment were not there), and a pile battered both ways past the geometry's limit. The 6-DOF tool says its loads act at the origin of the coordinates (the same off-centre input gave 341.7 kN in one tool and 1091.7 in the other with nothing said). The two calls with no printed page behind them are on the owner's revisit list (OWN-143, card parts 29 and 30). Detail: `module_work/gm319/CHECK.md`.
- **GM-325** 2026-11-13 (merged with GM-324, same branch and checks): site response through pystrata takes `water_table_depth` and `k0` for the mean effective stress behind the Darendeli and Menq curves and states both in the result when a layer used them (null with `stress_inputs_note` when none did; `defaults_used` lists them only then). The result carries the response with depth (`depth_profile`: strain, acceleration, initial and strain-compatible Vs by sub-layer) and each layer's stress (`layer_stresses`); where the arrays do not line up the profile is null with the reason. A linear run now reports its strain: `max_shear_strain_pct` was 0.0 on every linear run and is the peak of pystrata's strain history at each sub-layer's middle (checked against a closed form by hand; runs on pystrata 0.5 and 0.6). Bracketed duration, 0.0 for every record before, is computed only when a threshold is given (`bracketed_threshold_g`). No other value changes. Loose ends: GM-340.
- **GM-324** 2026-11-13 (merged from `fix/gm324-review`: the cloud build, an independent review, two rounds of fixes, a re-check; the whole suite green on GitHub, Python 3.13 with pystrata 0.6 included): the CPT tool returns what it is told to (`n_liquefiable`, `n_susceptible`, `lpi`, `lsn`, `ldi_m`, `min_fos`), and `field_correlations` returns its Vs, Dr, su ratio and Ic profiles, with `dr_min`/`dr_max` over the sand-like readings (Ic below the limit, returned as `i_c_limit`) and the su ratio's range over the clay-like ones. One total-stress rule on both SPT procedures: the sum of unit weight times thickness over the layers above each depth (the NCEER route's rule). ONE NUMBER CHANGES: on the B&I (2014) SPT procedure a profile whose unit weight varies with depth gets different stresses, CSR and factor of safety below the first depth (the manual's own example: minimum factor of safety 0.435 to 0.426); a profile with one unit weight is unchanged. Depths must be positive and increasing; the `depth` text says they are where (N1)60 was measured. NCEER refuses a first depth of 0 (it gave a factor of safety of 99.9). Layers above the water table still flagged liquefiable on the SPT routes: GM-339. Detail: `module_work/gm324/CHECK.md`.
- **GM-321** 2026-11-13 (merged with GM-320, same branch and checks): each braced support reports its own load from its `spacing` and `angle_deg` (load per support = load per metre x spacing; axial = that over cos(angle); vertical = axial x sin(angle); GEC-4 Eqs. 19 to 21; Caltrans Example 8-1 reproduced, 143.87 and 148.95 kips). `spacing` no longer defaults to 3 m: without it the loads stay per metre of wall. The vertical component of an inclined support is reported, not applied: the result and the calc package say it acts on the wall along its axis in the support's direction and that the wall is not checked for it (Caltrans Sec. 11-3.04, p. 11-12; GEC-4 App. A, p. A-13). A support's preload is refused (nothing used it), as is a spacing that is not a finite positive number. A wall's loads per metre, moment, shear and embedment are unchanged. The package's inputs table and two smaller loose ends are GM-338.
- **GM-320** 2026-11-13 (merged from `fix/gm320-review`: the cloud build, an independent review, two rounds of fixes each re-checked; the whole suite green on GitHub): Schmertmann layers must cover the strain influence diagram from the footing base to its bottom (2B square, 4B strip; NHI-06-089 Fig. 8-21a). A depth they leave out (below the deepest layer, a gap, above the first) is refused with its range, where it was counted as not settling with nothing said. `below_profile` (`schmertmann_below_profile` in the combined analysis and the calc package) says what lies there: `settled_by_another_method` (NHI-06-089 App. A.7, sand over clay), `incompressible`, or `last_layer_continued`; the result states it as a choice and a warning. Where the same call consolidates a layer in that depth only `settled_by_another_method` is taken, and a depth in neither list is named as not in the total. Depths within 10 mm count as covered. A `delta_sigmas` list that is not one number per layer is refused. A profile that covers the diagram gives the same settlement as before (checked against master). A depth given twice is still counted twice: GM-337. Detail: `module_work/gm320/CHECK.md`.

- **TA-39** 2026-11-13 (merged from `fix/ta-39-review`, master 8322130; cloud task A with its review fixes; the whole suite green on GitHub; unreleased; no setting): on a shared host two sign-in names no longer share an AI key or a folder. A name of letters, digits, `.`, `-` and `_` (no `_` at an end of the domain or of the user name, no `__`) keeps its key and its folder. Any other name (an apostrophe, a space, an accented letter) gets a folder of its own, `users/<old name>__<10 hex digits>/`, and an exact secret name, `PROMPTER-API-KEY--<TESTER>--<HASH>`, which the app looks up first; then that person's `PROMPTER-KEYS` entry; then the old secret name, so a key set up earlier still works. The app shows the exact name, never a key: in the refusal, in Connection diagnostics and from `python -m aec_document_review.tinyapps_keys` with the sign-in name. Earlier conversations stay in the old folder and are not moved, because another person may have shared it. A refused or spent key's vision-probe verdict is held for that caller's own model object only (120 s), so it no longer lowers the image size of other testers' looks. The lead's calls: for a name with punctuation the `PROMPTER-KEYS` entry is tried before the old secret (the exact entry beats the shared name); a name with `_` at an end of a part takes the hash too. Code `aec_document_review/identity.py` and `tinyapps_keys.py`, `aec_agent_core/vision_probe.py`; check and release note `module_work/ta39/CHECK.md`. The SharePoint folder follows TA-40's rule, on the same master: over 134,914 made-up sign-in names, 50,690 folder keys have 50,690 SharePoint owner folders, none shared, and no plain name's folder moves. Open elsewhere: the live checks (TA-42); the testers' names and the vault before the release (OWN-147); the host notes' key paragraph (INF-76); the running-turn table and the conversation-folder lookup keyed by folder and id (INF-71); GSE's frozen copies (`webapp/identity.py`, `webapp/tinyapps_keys.py`) keep both defects until INF-35. Noted, not fixed: a look-alike name with no key of its own is still served the old shared secret while it exists; a 403 counts as the caller's key refused, so a deployment that answers 403 to every key probes once per conversation in 120 s; a held verdict keeps its model object alive until the next lookup; a refused probe's error text is kept as it came, cut to 300 characters; `cached_profiles()` no longer lists a probe that failed on a refused key.

How to use it: CLAUDE.md, "Gate a branch on GitHub". GitHub rather than a cloud session: the repositories are public, so the runners cost nothing and no usage is spent. - **PL-36** 2026-11-10 (deployment review; a helper built it, the lead read the diff): four planlens fixes merged to planlens main (merge de93bc1; commits 3cce550, 99a9b04, 09e61aa, 8e05df3), unreleased; they reach the app with planlens 0.15.1 (OWN-134). A marked copy never replaces the document that was opened or a file the app did not write (it goes beside it, and the result's `saved_elsewhere` says which file and why; `remove` on a file with no mark of the app's is refused). Exact search compares what a reader sees: a ligature as its letters, any run of white space or a no-break space as one space, a word hyphenated at a line end with or without its hyphen; a hit still returns the page's own text and box. A search with many hits returns the first that fit, with how many were left out and how to narrow it, where it returned an over-limit error. A password-protected, empty, damaged or page-less PDF is refused at open in plain words with what the user can do. Whole planlens suite 2142 passed; the app's Document Review, core and document-tool tests pass against it (two app tests stood a file with no mark of the app's in for an earlier marked copy; fixed, b071ac7). Noted, not fixed: a regular expression that can match empty text makes `Document.search` raise; only search's result is measured whole, the structure, markups and page-map results still reserve a flat amount; the bare toolkit and `write_markups` with append on still add to any existing PDF named as output, as documented. *Src:* `module_work/docreview_review_2026-11-10/REVIEW.md` §1.6
- **TA-36** 2026-11-10 (the owner's ask: a deployment review of Document Review and the code under it; six read-only reviewers, every finding checked against the code; `module_work/docreview_review_2026-11-10/REVIEW.md`): fixed on master, each with a test of made-up values, unreleased (OWN-134). *One process, several testers:* a request with no sign-in header on the App Service is refused with a message (it was served as "anonymous", sharing one folder and the shared settings, and `DEV_IDENTITY` was read there); no Working folder box for a tester the proxy named, and a stored or restored working folder is not used on a shared host; a restored zip's working folder is dropped and a file reference never leaves the conversation folder; a file path quoted in a tool result is copied in only from the working or temporary folder; `find_in_working_folder` no longer follows "../"; the wrapper turns model-written plotting code off on the App Service (OWN-133). An independent reviewer then read the branch and found three more of the kind in older code, fixed too: a restored zip's `attachments.json` could name any server file, which was read and handed to the agent; a zip restored on a shared host kept its id, so two people could hold one conversation id and the second to open it took over the first's folder and running turn (a restore there is now a copy with an id of its own, unless the person still has that conversation); the temporary folder, which is everybody's, is no longer a source of reported files on a shared host. A sign-in name is now whatever Windows allows (an apostrophe or an accented letter no longer locks its owner out). *SharePoint:* a refused upload raises (the mirror had counted it as saved and never sent it again); a throttled call waits and is sent again, a 401 gets a new token once; a throttle that outlives its sends, or names a wait over 20 s, fails the call and holds later calls unsent until it is over, so a mirror does not wait out every remaining file under the store's lock; error texts drop an upload session's address query; the refusal check no longer fires on a file name with 403 in it and is worded for the host. *The turn:* a pass the model-call budget ended is the turn's answer (auto-continue had given it a new budget, up to three in a turn); two error messages say what to do; finished turns leave the process's table. *Files and settings:* a control character in text for Word or Excel no longer loses the file; Excel text that opens with "=" is written as text and the result says so; a control character in a sheet name or inside a Markdown table row no longer loses the workbook or cuts the table; a document-tools setting that is not a number falls back to its default; Connection diagnostics on Tiny Apps (the upload probe's expected refusal, the streaming setting's name, tokens scrubbed from the report). *The wrapper:* a failed install is said and stops the start unless an earlier install is in place; the published address is the app setting `APP_ORIGINS` (each address taken as written), with a warning while unset. Noted, not fixed (small, or seen only by reading): feedback files appended with no lock; `calculate` raises Python's recursion limit process-wide per call; a new HTTP client per engine build, never closed; the SharePoint error log read and rewritten whole; search reads one page of results; three of four SharePoint settings hide the storage block instead of naming the fourth; the mirror never removes a file; no size limit on an image sent to the model; the upload box on the Databricks route has no total limit; on the `minimal` measuring build a helper's budget marks the whole turn; a piece of a large upload sent again after a 503 fails the upload if the first send had landed (as before); no test yet for the host-worded refusal text or for an upload sent again on a 503. Open from it: TA-37 to TA-41, DR-41, DR-42, VIS-30, PL-36 to PL-38, INF-71; INF-16, TA-24, TA-03, DR-06, DR-07, DR-25 and DR-38 added to. Commits b159fba, a7a54c2 and the notes commit after them (branch `review/docreview-fable`).
